held-out benchmark.

### C-ArmLSpine: case134 not built, case39's third frame dropped, case118's oblique called a lateral (2026-09-23)

Audit rows 34, 77 and 872, and the verdict at line 869. Jobs **40416561** (SPINEPS/levels, GPU), **40416562**
(poses, GPU), **40416563** (bank rebuild, census, anonymity; CPU, `afterany` on both). Recorded in
`SchwingNet/results/JOBS_data_repairs.txt`.

**What was wrong.** A spot frame was being found by folder and file extension rather than by what it is. `case134`
keeps its AP (0.0 deg, 77 kVp) and lateral (90.0 deg, 89 kVp) inside `case134/CBCT/`, so a walker that looked only
in `C-arm/` returned nothing and the case was dropped — and then explained, in the DATASHEET, in Table 1 footnote
f, in `NUMBERS.md` and in `methods_sources.md`, as a case that "ships no AP or lateral frame". `case39` ships three
spot frames and `carmlspine_labels._frames()` kept the first per view by code, with no comment and no written rule.
`case118`'s second frame is at **-58.1 deg** — an oblique — and the view rule in force at the 20:47 build ("AP if
|angle| < 45, else LAT") called it a lateral; the `OBL` class landed at 21:20 in commit 0b0424d and no bank was
rebuilt, so that frame is scored as a lateral in every cross-validation and test number, and `NUMBERS.md` line 94
("C-ArmLSpine exactly AP or lateral") is false.

**The repair.**
- `tools/carmlspine_io.read_frames` now walks the WHOLE case tree and decides by **DICOM Modality and image size**:
  Modality XA with >= 2048 rows is a spot exposure, wherever it sits and whatever it is called; Modality XA with
  1024 rows is a cone-beam sweep projection and is not a frame. `spot_frames` returns every one of them with a
  unique view tag, so a repeat exposure is exported (`case39_LAT2`) instead of being dropped.
- The view class comes from `view_of_angle` (AP <= 20 deg, LAT within 20 deg of 90, otherwise OBL) **for every
  case**, so no other frame can be mis-typed.
- The oblique has no device convention of its own. `carmlspine_pose.oblique_starts` turns the AP consensus
  convention about the patient's cranio-caudal axis by the frame's own recorded `PositionerPrimaryAngle`, in both
  senses (the header does not say which way the positioner counts), and records the winner with its margin. The
  stale `case118_LAT*` poses are moved to `pose/retired/`.
- Box convention: the vertebral **body** in every frame. Where SPINEPS named the level, the instance is chosen by
  its VerSe id as before; where it did not (case134), the instance is chosen by which SPINEPS instance the
  TotalSegmentator vertebra overlaps most — naming and box selection are separate questions, and a vertebra nobody
  could name still has a body SPINEPS segmented. `box_source` records which, and `summary.json` now counts every
  box by source, so the share off-convention can never again be invisible.
- case134's levels: VERIDAH labelled its lumbosacral CBCT **C1-C5 + sacrum** while TotalSegmentator reads L1-L5 +
  sacrum, so the relabel refused the mapping and no `_veridah` mask was written, and `dual_seg_compare` recorded
  `"no SPINEPS mask"` — false, the mask exists. levels.json is rebuilt from a table that falls back to the plain
  VerSe-id mask, so case134 becomes a real *disagree* record with `level: null`: two frames that carry body boxes
  for detection and no level names. A second SPINEPS reading is run into a side directory to say whether that
  labelling is stable; it is reported either way, not guessed at.

**Verification.** `carmlspine/qc/FRAMES_CHECK.md` (copied to `SchwingNet/results/repairs/CARMLSPINE_CHECK.md`) puts
the release census beside the bank: the number of spot frames the release holds, by view class and by the folder
each was found in, against `summary.json`. The repair is complete when **release minus bank = 0**. Expected: 299
spot frames over 149 cases (297 in `C-arm/` + case134's 2 in `CBCT/`) = 149 AP + 149 LAT + 1 OBL, against the 296
the bank held. The oblique construction is checked outside the registration by
`tools/carmlspine_oblique_selftest.py`: at 90 deg it reproduces the lateral convention the 296-frame consensus
picked independently and at 0 deg the AP convention, exactly (max |dR|, |dt| and source shift all 0.0e+00), and the
two senses give opposite laterals — **PASS**.

**Anonymisation.** case134's `/ori` series and CBCT still carry a real PatientID. `carmlspine_frame_census.py anon`
reads the identifier strings from the release's own headers and checks every exported file NAME and every exported
text BYTE (frame bank and pose directory) against them, reporting counts only; an identifier is never printed,
logged or written. Exported records now carry `frame_key`, a digest of the frame's basename, in place of the
basename itself, because a third of the release names frames by UID. A non-zero count exits the job non-zero.

**Not done here.** The 602 raw cone-beam sweep projections (`case134/ori` 301 + `case39/CBCT` 301) are a separate
item (A24) and are explicitly not exported. Every number scored on the old bank — the fixed-split test cells, the
5-fold and day-grouped cross-validation, the 4,276 real-frame total, the 148/296 in DATASHEET sections 2.1, 3 and
8, `NUMBERS.md` 94/101/118, `methods_sources.md` and Table 1 footnote f — is computed on 296 frames with case118's
oblique scored as a lateral and must be restated once this bank lands; the pre-repair bank is kept at
`realxray/carmlspine_pre_repair_2026-09-23` so the two can be compared rather than merely replaced.

### 2026-09-23 — COBRA counted up to four times in one detector arm (jobs 40416536, 40416560, 40416578)

**The gap.** Row 18 / line 120 above: the derived banks `cobra_elekta_v2`, `cobra_elekta_ap45` and
`cobra_elekta_v2_ap45` are separate directories under `realxray/`, and `tools/realxray_train.py` `ready()` globbed
`realxray/*/data.yaml` and excluded only names containing `smoke` or ending `_multi`. Any pooled or lodo arm
launched after 2026-09-22 00:51 therefore treated four renderings of one Elekta scanner's frames as four machines.

**Measured, from the lists on disk rather than from the code that wrote them.** `tools/audit_realxray_lists.py`
reads each run's own `data.yaml`, resolves every image directory, and maps every image back to the independent
bank it came from. `SchwingNet/results/realxray_lists_audit.json`; grid copy
`/rs/rs_grp_oschome/go2432/tmp/realxray_lists_audit.json`; log `/rs/rs_grp_oschome/go2432/tmp/rxlists_40416578.out`.

| arm | train images | distinct frames | duplicate | val images | distinct | duplicate | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| rx_lodo_carmlspine_1024 | 3,094 | 3,094 | 0 | 752 | 752 | 0 | clean |
| rx_lodo_cobra_elekta_1024 | 502 | 502 | 0 | 160 | 160 | 0 | clean |
| rx_lodo_deepfluoro_1024 | 3,072 | 3,072 | 0 | 704 | 704 | 0 | clean |
| rx_only_carmlspine_1024 | 210 | 210 | 0 | 30 | 30 | 0 | clean |
| rx_only_carmlspine_multi_1024 | 3,660 | 3,660 | 0 | 565 | 565 | 0 | clean; its bank is derived by design, and `only:` is the one arm that may name a derived bank |
| rx_pooled_1024 | 3,088 | 3,088 | 0 | 246 | 246 | 0 | clean |
| **rx_pooled_1024_init18** | **8,101** | **3,088** | **5,013** | **584** | **246** | **338** | **duplicated** |

**One arm, and one only.** `rx_pooled_1024_init18` (data.yaml 2026-09-22 05:57, weights 10:33) is the single arm
trained after the derived banks were built. Its COBRA train images are 2,640 + 1,238 + 2,568 + 1,207 = 7,653 for
2,640 distinct exposures; 2,445 of the 5,013 duplicate train images are byte-identical (the `ap45` banks are
symlink views, so the very same file is listed twice), and the rest are those same exposures re-windowed into new
PNGs. The six earlier arms were built between 2026-09-21 22:00 and 23:53, before the derived banks existed, and
the census confirms 0 duplicates in every one of them — read off their lists, not inferred from their dates.

**What it is NOT, checked rather than assumed.** It is not a split leak. All four COBRA `splits.json` are
byte-identical (md5 `d2ad1ad23e4d006873f1ee26719c58f9`), every derived bank carries its parent's split, no case id
appears in both a train list and a val list of any arm, and the census reports `leaks=0`. The defect is that 118
COBRA training patients were weighted up to four times against C-ArmLSpine's 105 and DeepFluoro's 4 — not that a
test patient was trained on.

**A second consequence the gap row did not state.** `init18`'s `scores.json` reports `cobra_elekta/test`,
`cobra_elekta_ap45/test`, `cobra_elekta_v2/test` and `cobra_elekta_v2_ap45/test`: **one test set of 27 patients
scored four times**. Any mean over that arm's "datasets" gives the Elekta device weight 4 and every other device
weight 1. (A lodo arm scores its held-out device's train, val and test — three splits of one device, which is that
arm's definition. The census counts (device, split) pairs so the two cannot be mistaken for each other.)

**Reported numbers affected: none.** Every detector number in `SchwingNet/` comes from `rx_lodo_carmlspine_1024`
(NUMBERS.md 109 and 113, atlas/model.json, atlas/dataset.json 160 and 199, COUNTING_CHECK.md, COUPLING.md,
slurm/coupling.sh), `rx_lodo_deepfluoro_1024`, `rx_lodo_cobra_elekta_1024` or `rx_pooled_1024` — all clean above.
`init18` appears in no results file. Its only mention is a plan: `docs/HANDOFF_2026-09-21_PM.md:563` calls it "the
clean DeepFluoro same-device number". **That sentence must not be quoted from the existing weights**; the number it
promises has to come from a retrain.

**The fix, and why this form.** `tools/realxray_banks.json` is now an admission list, and `ready()` **refuses** a
directory that is not in it, naming the directory. A `derived: true` key in each bank's own `data.yaml` was the
obvious alternative and was rejected: the absence of such a key is indistinguishable from "independent", so the
next builder that forgets to write it reproduces this defect exactly, silently, again. Absence from a registry is
distinguishable, because the registry is the thing being read. A name rule (`endswith("_v2")`) was rejected for the
same reason — a name rule is what failed here. And the registry is checked against something outside itself:
`assert_disjoint()` reads the case ids off disk and refuses any arm in which two admitted banks share one, whatever
the registry claims. `tools/realxray_train.py check` is the gate; `tools/test_realxray_banks.py` carries ten tests,
including the exact failure (an unclassified `_v2` directory appears and is refused) and two banks sharing a case.

**A bug found while building it.** `splits.json` is not one schema. `deepfluoro/splits.json` also carries `rule` (a
string), `md5_bucket` and `frames` (dicts) and `loso` (a list of dicts). The idiomatic
`{c for v in splits.values() for c in v}` raises on `loso` — and had `loso` not been there it would have iterated
the 300-character `rule` string **character by character into 300 one-letter case ids** and compared them happily.
`case_ids()` now reads the keys `train`/`val`/`test` only and refuses a non-string entry; two tests cover it.

**A latent instance of the same root cause, fixed.** `tools/data_lineage.py` `scan_realxray()` keyed its canonical
real-frame ids by directory name (`frame:{ds}/{case}/{stem}`), and all four COBRA banks ship their own
`meta/*.json` (3,480 / 3,480 / 1,634 / 1,634). Any total over that section would have counted 10,228 COBRA real
frames instead of 3,480, and would have read one patient as four people. It is keyed by the root bank now, from the
same registry. No number from that tool has ever been quoted — no lineage output exists on disk — so nothing
published moves.

**Not done, deliberately.** Nothing was retrained. `rx_pooled_1024_init18` is left on disk with this note against
it; the pooled arm is rebuilt at G5 on the final corpus, which does not exist yet (COBRA is still fetching, job
40416315). The four derived banks are left on disk too: `cobra_elekta_ap45` / `_v2_ap45` implement a written
scientific criterion (COBRA laterals through a living pelvis are photon-starved, `tools/realxray_subset_view.py`)
and `_v2` a written display fix, so they remain useful as `only:` arms — they must simply never be a device. Two
smaller things this census exposed and did not repair, for whoever owns those rows: `cobra_elekta_v2_ap45/subset.json`
records `frames.train 1238` while 1,207 files are on disk (it copied its grandparent's count, and v2 had already
dropped the four collimated cases); and `cobra_elekta_v2/meta` holds 3,480 JSONs for 3,384 images, the extra 96
being those same collimated frames.

**How it will be verified.** `python tools/realxray_train.py check --root /rs/rs_grp_oschome/go2432/data/realxray`
prints `PASS` with the admitted device list; `python tools/test_realxray_banks.py --root ...` prints
`REALXRAY_BANKS_TEST PASS failures=0` with `t_real_root` among the tests; `sbatch slurm/audit_realxray_lists.sh`
prints `REALXRAY_LIST_AUDIT_DONE arms=<n> dirty=<n> leaks=<n>`. The repair holds when a re-run after the full COBRA
re-export prints `dirty=0` for every arm trained from that point, and it fails loudly — not silently — the first
time a new bank appears under `realxray/` without a line in `tools/realxray_banks.json`.

**Downstream consumers that will silently drop the new frames.** A third view class and a repeat exposure are new
shapes for this corpus, and five places still iterate over the pair `("AP", "LAT")`. None of them will raise; each
will quietly leave the oblique or the repeat out, which is the exact failure mode this project keeps meeting. They
are listed rather than changed here, because the corpus build and the device card belong to other open items:

- `tools/devcorpus_build.py:93` — `if (case, view) in seen or view not in ("AP", "LAT"): continue` drops
  `case118_OBL` and `case39_LAT2` from the Surgio-B51 pose corpus.
- `tools/device_fit.py:1300` — same filter, and its per-case output name is keyed on the view, so `case39_LAT2`
  collides with `case39_LAT` and is skipped. It *will* now pick up `case134`'s AP and lateral, because it calls
  `CIO.read_frames`: the Surgio-B51 device card's input set changes and the card should be refitted.
- `tools/carmlspine_eval.py:134` and `tools/carmlspine_pool_scores.py:58` — per-view cells exist for AP and LAT
  only, so an oblique lands in `overall` and in no view cell.
- `tools/carmlspine_pose_qc.py:51` — the same.
- `SchwingNet/tools/fig2_flow.py:52` still draws "149 cases released / 1 failed conversion / 148 cases, 296 frames
  (148 AP + 148 lateral)". Every clause of that box is now wrong.

## Repairs

### 2026-09-23 — TotalSegmentator: the SPINEPS gap (110 of 1,228 v2 CTs, and v3 never benched)

Rows repaired: *"SPINEPS+VERIDAH bench: 1,228 v2 CTs -> 1,118 with a seg-vert mask"* (class **bug**, "72 whole
spines never diagnosed") and the v3.0.0 half of *"v3.0.0 release -> disk (the download started 2026-09-19)"*
(flagged as possibly unfinished). The masks are also what makes a rendered box a VERTEBRAL BODY box, so this
row is on the critical path of A28 and gate G4, not only of G0.

Jobs (all in `SchwingNet/results/JOBS_data_repairs.txt`): **40416538** diagnosis; **40416605** the v2/VerSe
retry (supersedes 40416539, cancelled after its first failures showed the cause); **40416540** SPINEPS over
v3.0.0; **40416612** body masks for both; **40416613** the post-repair report; **40416614** the diagnosis
rerun with two defects of its own fixed. New code: `tools/ts_spineps_failure_classes.py`,
`tools/ts_spineps_diagnose.py`, `tools/ts_v3_link_reused.py`, `benchmark/run_spineps_retry.py`,
`slurm/{ts_spineps_diagnose,spineps_ts_retry,spineps_ts_v3,body_masks_ts}.sh`. Nothing touches job 40416509
(SPINEPS on CTSpinoPelvic1K), 40416510 (body masks) or `benchmark/run_spineps_cases.py`, which 40416509 is
using; the retry has its own runner.

**What was established, and against what.** The runner's timing log holds the exception for every failure and
had never been read; it gives five classes. The anatomy was then decided NOT from those exceptions but from
each release's OWN vertebra segmentations — made by a different network (TotalSegmentator's nnU-Net; VerSe's
expert masks) — counting vertebra voxels per level beside every CT that came back empty. The floor for "there
is a level here to name" is 3,000 voxels in the largest single level: a quarter of one whole vertebra at the
1.5 mm isotropic grid both releases ship (a whole vertebra is 25–40 mL, the body alone about 3.4 mL).

| | n | |
|---|---|---|
| **bug** — the release labels a real spine, SPINEPS failed on it | **62** | 53 already carry a render in `ts_drr`/`ts_drr_v3`; 8 are VerSe volumes of 5–24 labelled levels (verse809: 24 levels, 1,676,460 vertebra voxels) |
| **criterion** — no spine in the field | **54** | 36 label no vertebra voxel at all (the same scans `ts_to_drr` skips for "no vertebra labelled"); 18 hold only clipped corners, largest level 249–2,560 voxels |
| unknown | 0 | (the first run reported 8; that was a defect in the diagnosis tool, below) |

This replaces the audit's "110 TS (+8 VerSe): 38 … expected, 72 … crashed on" with a count decided from disk.

**The crashes, the explanation that failed, and the ladder.** The three crash classes are 22 × `assert_affine
… shape mismatch (129,555,77),(129,555,76)` at `spineps/phase_labeling.py:184`, 21 × cc3d `Statistics can only
be computed … Max: 8` from TPTBox `np_volume`, and 15 × `bbox_nd: img is empty` at TPTBox
`nii_wrapper.py:867 compute_crop`, called with `VIBE_CROP_MARGIN_MM`. **The tidy explanation was that all
three come from the body segmentation SPINEPS runs before the semantic model, and that `--no-crop` would clear
all three. It was tested and it is wrong.** `--no-crop` sets `proc_sem_crop_input`, a different crop:
TSV3_s0122 raised the identical `bbox_nd` traceback with the flag and without it. And the shape mismatch is
not in a crop at all — it is the VERIDAH labelling phase comparing the image against the instance mask. What
does clear that class is the isotropic resample: TSV3_s0001 and TSV3_s0208 failed native and `--no-crop` and
both came back on a 1.5 mm grid.

`benchmark/run_spineps_retry.py` therefore climbs all four rungs — native → native `--no-crop` → 1.5 mm
isotropic resample → isotropic `--no-crop` — records which one produced each mask in `<case>_retry.json`, and
leaves the per-class answer to be read off the result rather than argued for. Only the last two touch a voxel
of the released CT, and those map the masks back onto the CT's own shape and affine (nearest neighbour,
nothing reoriented) so `tools/body_masks.py` and `make_drr_dataset --body-mask-dir` accept them. A case that
fails all four lands in `<case>_failed.json` with all four exceptions.

**v3.0.0 is completely downloaded — the flag is refuted, not excused.** Zenodo record 22688904 lists exactly
one file; the zip on disk matches it in bytes (37,415,622,057) and in md5 (`0728beea76475e92cdb3160e228265d1`),
and all 1,939 subject directories carry a `ct.nii.gz` and a `segmentations/`.

**v3 was never benched; it is now, without paying twice.** v3 re-releases most of v2 with refined
SEGMENTATIONS and the same CT images, so 1,118 of the 1,939 v3 subjects have a `ct.nii.gz` byte-identical to a
v2 CT that already has a mask. Those were linked under the v3 case names (`tools/ts_v3_link_reused.py`,
3,348 links) and SPINEPS runs on the remaining 821. **The join is by md5, never by `s####`**: v201 s1406–s1429
are different people in v300, and a name join there would hand one person's masks to another.

**Two defects in the diagnosis tool itself, found and fixed before its numbers were used** (40416614 reran it):
VerSeFusion's unified tree names the expert mask `<scan>_msk.nii.gz`, not `*seg-vert*`, so all eight VerSe
cases first came back "unknown" for want of a glob; and the no-spine floor was first written against the TOTAL
vertebra voxels rather than the largest single level, which called a scan with two 200-voxel fragments a spine.

**How it will be verified.** `/rs/rs_grp_oschome/go2432/data/spineps/ts_diagnose/ts_spineps_final.{json,md}`
(job 40416613, chained `afterany` behind the retry and the v3 run) recomputes the table above against the
post-repair disk: a row that remains is a scan still absent, with its release vertebra count and all four
exceptions beside it. Marker lines: `TSRETRY_RC=`, `TSV3_RC=`, `BODYMASK_TS_RC=`,
`TS_SPINEPS_DIAGNOSE_DONE`. The row closes when every remaining absence is a **criterion** row.

**Not repaired here, and why.** `ts_to_drr.py` reads the RELEASE's vertebra labels, not SPINEPS's, so a scan
the release leaves unlabelled still cannot be rendered from it and no new render follows from these masks; the
72 spines at issue were already rendered. Whether `ts_to_drr.py` should take SPINEPS as its label source (it
would recover renders and give L6/T13 names TotalSegmentator's class list cannot express) is a G4 decision,
not a repair. Likewise the 18 rendered scans whose largest labelled level is under 3,000 voxels are in the
render bank as near-empty images; that is a render-bank question, recorded here and not acted on.

### 2026-09-23 — CTSpinoPelvic1K record 0533, the truncated CT in the public release

Jobs **40416624** (`slurm/ctsp0533_repair.sh`, the repair and the swap), **40416604**
(`slurm/ctsp0533_bone_check.sh`) and **40416617** (`slurm/ctsp0533_series_check.sh`, the two
checks that decided it), then **40416625** (SPINEPS + body mask), **40416626** (the CPU render
banks), **40416627** (physics and the film banks) and **40416638** (the lstv_flag renders).
Earlier attempts 40416534 and 40416542 failed in the environment and are recorded with their
causes in `SchwingNet/results/JOBS_data_repairs.txt`. Work tree
`/rs/rs_grp_oschome/go2432/data/ctsp0533_repair/`.

**The repair.** `ct/0533_ct.nii.gz` was rebuilt from TCIA with the release's own
`fetch_from_tcia.py` — the SeriesInstanceUID `manifest.json` names for the record, 577 slices,
dcm2niix, resampled onto `labels/0533_label.nii.gz` — and swapped into
`~/data/CTSpinoPelvic1K/ct/` by atomic rename once every gate passed. **17,301,504 B sha256
`20e673dc…d6e23` (fails `gzip -t`) → 250,067,995 B sha256 `478a74e9…a164d5`**, shape
(512, 577, 512), PIR, affine identical to the label, float32 like its neighbours. The truncated
file is kept at `…/ctsp0533_repair/0533_ct.TRUNCATED.20e673dc6c2a.nii.gz`; the release directory
now holds 802 CTs and every one passes `gzip -t`.

**The gate that failed first, and why it was the gate and not the volume.** The rebuild reads
75.7% of labelled voxels at or above 200 HU against the 85.1 the manifest records for this
record — a 9.4-point gap, which is exactly the shape of a wrong-series rebuild (every patient in
this cohort was scanned twice). It is not. `spine_bone_pct` is measured over the VERTEBRA
identifiers only, which is written down nowhere. Ten records whose CT is intact, measured from
their released CTs under the identical computation (40416604), sit 2 to 5 points BELOW their own
recorded value over all labels and reproduce it to between +0.20 and +0.27 of a point over the
vertebrae. 0533 rebuilt: **85.32 against 85.1, delta +0.22, inside the control range.** The v10
and v11 labels give identical fractions, so the label version is not in it. Independently,
40416617 compared the downloaded series against the manifest's own description of the record —
manufacturer, model, reconstruction kernel, slice thickness, kVp, sex, age band and the series
identifier — and all eight agree. The verifier now measures the vertebra-restricted fraction with
a 1.0-point tolerance and states the all-label figure beside it.

**A second defect found on the way, not repaired.** `manifest.json`'s `n_voxels_fg` does not
describe the shipped labels: for 0533 it says 2,094,598 where the released mask holds 3,167,246,
and it is 0 in 340 of 802 records. It is now advisory in the verifier rather than a gate, and it
is written into the release's `KNOWN_ISSUES.md`. Recomputing that column is a manifest rebuild
and is not done here.

**The banks.** 0533 is being rendered into `~/ctsp_drr` (`make_drr_dataset --resume`),
`~/anchor_drr` and `~/anchor_drr_c2` (`make_anchor_drr --cases 0533`, a new flag that renders only
the named ids and leaves a finished bank's `data.yaml` and `build_stats` alone), `~/physics_drr`
with the parameters that bank was built with, `~/ctsp_drr_film` and `~/physics_drr_film`, and
`~/lstv_flag/renders`. `physics_rare` is untouched: 0533 is `lstv_label` normal and was never
eligible for the rare oversample. **These banks box whole vertebrae**, and the convention the
paper adopts is the vertebral BODY, so the durable half of this repair is job 40416625, which puts
0533 through SPINEPS and `tools/body_masks.py` so the G4 body-box rebuild covers 802 of 802 rather
than 801.

**How to verify.** `gzip -t ~/data/CTSpinoPelvic1K/ct/0533_ct.nii.gz`; the gate report at
`…/ctsp0533_repair/logs/verify_0533.json` (`all_pass: true`, nine gates); the control table at
`…/logs/bone_check_0533.json`; the series table at `…/logs/series_check_0533.json`; and the marker
lines `CTSP0533_REPAIR_RC=0 swapped=1 count=802`, `CTSP0533_SPINEPS_RC=…`, `CTSP0533_RENDERS_RC=…`,
`CTSP0533_PHYSICS_RC=…`, `CTSP0533_LSTVFLAG_RC=…` in `~/model_night/logs/`.

**Still open, and it needs Greg.** The public release is still broken. Nothing was pushed to
HuggingFace in this workflow; until the command below runs, every consumer of
`OpenSpineConsortium/CTSpinoPelvic1K` gets the truncated object.

The push is written and uploaded to the grid as `slurm/ctsp0533_hf_push.sh` and **was deliberately
not submitted**. It refuses unless the local file's sha256 is `478a74e9…a164d5` and it passes
`gzip -t`, uploads `ct/0533_ct.nii.gz` to `OpenSpineConsortium/CTSpinoPelvic1K` on `main` with the
OSC token, and reads the object back from the API to confirm the stored identifier is the new
sha256 and not `20e673dc…d6e23`. One command:

    wsl -e bash -lc 'ssh go2432@grid.wayne.edu "sbatch -D ~/spinesurg-ct-nnunet slurm/ctsp0533_hf_push.sh"'

The `KNOWN_ISSUES.md` entry (section 10, naming the bad sha and the date) is written in the
dataset repo at `data/zenodo_deposit/KNOWN_ISSUES.md` and belongs in the same release update; the
Zenodo deposit carries the same file and needs it too.

### 2026-09-23 — COBRA2026 Varian: the patient→RTK axis map, solved and checked against the authors' own render

Row repaired: *"COBRA2026 Varian centres F and G: 26 cases fetched pre-analysis, 225 public, 0 exported, 0 in any
model"* (class **unfinished**). `tools/realxray_cobra_export.py` returned `manufacturer Varian: Varian geometry not
solved (out of scope)` for every one of them. Jobs **40416546** (`slurm/cobra_varian_geom.sh`, 12 shards, 99 Varian
cases on disk + A002 and A004 as positive controls), **40416618** (`slurm/cobra_varian_confirm.sh`) and the banded
re-run **40416639**; new tool `tools/cobra_varian_geometry.py`.

**The answer. The Varian map is `[[1, 0, 0], [0, 0, -1], [0, -1, 0]]`** — RTK world x = +patient x (left),
RTK world y = −patient z (caudal), RTK world z = −patient y (anterior) — and it is **not** the Elekta map
`[[0, 1, 0], [0, 0, -1], [1, 0, 0]]`, which ranks 5th to 14th of 16 on these cases. Every Varian case scored so far
returns the same map, on both targets. The two maps differ by exactly a 90° rotation about the rotation axis and
nothing else (both have determinant −1, the same handedness convention): at RTK gantry 0 the Varian kV source is
anterior to the patient, where an on-board imager sits under IEC, while the Elekta file's angle is the MV gantry
angle with the kV source 90° away. That reading is consistent with the hardware; it is not what the answer rests on.

**What it rests on.** Two targets, one of them outside anything we compute. (1) `projections_simulated.mha`, the
authors' own DRR of the same `ct_def.mha` through the same `geometry.xml`: our render under the winning map scores
edge NCC **0.93–0.97** against it (log-intensity NCC 0.998), against 0.29–0.55 for the runner-up. (2) the real
projections: log-intensity NCC **+0.85 to +0.97**, and edge NCC 0.60–0.81 once the craniocaudal blade shadow is
excluded. **Positive control:** the same procedure returns the Elekta map for A002 (edge real +0.822, margin
+0.351, sim +0.883) and A004 (+0.754, +0.288, +0.898) — if it had not, the procedure would be what is broken.
**Before the search, the convention is checked rather than assumed:** every matrix's camera centre must sit at
SID·(sin gantry, 0, cos gantry) — RTK's IEC convention with the rotation axis along world y — and a case whose
matrices do not is refused, because then no signed permutation of the patient axes could be the answer. Both
vendors pass it to under a millimetre.

**A criterion that was wrong, and the fix.** The first pass scored the real frames over the whole detector and
flagged 4 of the first 7 Varian cases "unsolved" on a margin of 0.04–0.09. That was the rule failing, not the
geometry: on the 86 × 43 cm imager the craniocaudal blades are a hard edge in every real frame and are not in the
CT, so they swamp the gradient score. Job 40416618 measured it instead of asserting it — F011 edge NCC 0.164 over
the whole frame, **0.598 over the exposed band**, rising to 0.810 at a 4.5 mm block while the runner-up stays at
0.418; F010 (no blades) 0.613 → 0.735 with block size, runner-up 0.238 → 0.437. The real-frame score is now taken
over the exposed band, exactly as the Elekta path already does, and the written rule is: **the authors' simulation
decides (margin ≥ 0.10) and the real frames must independently rank the same map first**; the real-frame margin is
reported, not required, because it carries the imaging chain's noise and collimation and the simulation does not.

**Three Varian imaging chains, not one** (`ImagerSizeX` over the 225 public cases: 122 / 26 / 77), each internally
consistent across three independent fields — the .mha spacing, `metadata.yaml`, and the vendor's own detector model
name: **PaxScan4030CB** 1024 × 768 at 0.388 mm (397 × 298 mm, SDD 1500, half fan, detector offset −160 mm, full
trajectory, ~894 frames); **RTI4343iL** 1536 × 384 at 0.28 × 1.12 mm (430 × 430 mm, SDD 1500, full fan, ~499
frames); **RTI8643iL** 3072 × 384 at 0.28 × 1.12 mm (860 × 430 mm, SDD 1540, full fan, half trajectory, ~407
frames). The 86 cm detector is real, not a placeholder spacing: the model name says 8643, and 860 mm at SDD 1540 is
the 538 mm reconstruction field `metadata.yaml` asks for. Consequences for the export: its `anisotropic detector
pixels` exclusion must go and x and y must be resampled separately (4:1 pixels on 103 of 225 cases), and the
collimation handling the Elekta path has must apply to the wide chain.

**Noticed, not repaired.** `docs/devices/varian_halcyon.json` on the grid (no copy in the checkout) mixes two of
these chains: pixel pitch 0.388 mm with panel 397 × 298 mm and image size 1024 × 768 — the PaxScan — against
`SID_mm: 1540`, which is the RTI8643iL's. Its `orientation_convention` field still says "the patient→RTK map
settled on Elekta is used first"; that map is now known to be wrong for Varian by 90°. Whatever that card was
fitted on must be re-read before a Varian card is quoted (CLAIM_CHECKLIST row 103).

**Box convention.** Greg, 2026-09-23: the human annotation boxes vertebral bodies, so every CT that needs boxes for
the DRRs goes through SPINEPS. The Varian planning CTs had no segmentation of any kind, because the export refused
them. Jobs **40416564** (ct_def.mha → NIfTI, geometry read back), **40416565** (SPINEPS, 4 GPU shards →
`/rs/.../data/spineps/cobra_varian`) and **40416566** (`tools/body_masks.py` over both COBRA vendors →
`/rs/.../data/spineps/bodies_cobra`, a separate directory from job 40416510, which is untouched) fill it, and the
146 Elekta cases that already had SPINEPS get body masks in the same pass.

**How it will be verified.** `VARIAN_GEOM_DONE solved=N` in `~/model_night/logs/vgeom_40416639_*.out` and
`varian/VARIAN_GEOMETRY.json` (cases, solved, the map histogram — it must hold one map for all Varian cases —
`agree_on_both_targets`, and the median edge NCC per imaging chain). Then the acceptance test a number cannot give:
job **40416620** draws the per-level **vertebral body** boxes of the planning CT through the solved geometry onto
real frames (`varian/proof/<case>_<frame>_bodyboxes.png`, with A002 as the control). A correct geometry puts the
boxes on the bones. Until that picture has been looked at, this repair is not finished, and no Varian frame goes
into a bank.

**Why nothing caught it.** `SHA256SUMS.txt` in the deposit has 810 lines — 802 labels and eight
documents — and **no entry for any CT**, because the deposit ships labels and the CTs live on
HuggingFace. So the half of the release that is 193 GB is the half with no published checksum,
and `upload_large_folder` verifies nothing about gzip integrity. A `gzip -t` sweep over `ct/`
costs minutes and is the check that would have caught this at push time.

**The picture, looked at (03:20).** Job **40416644** (`slurm/cobra_varian_bone.sh`, `cobra_varian_geometry.py bone`)
draws the CT's bone path length through the solved map as a contour on the real frame, next to the same contour
under the runner-up map — the check that needs no segmentation, so it did not wait for the GPU queue. On the
Elekta control A002 at gantry 270° the red contour traces the sacrum, the pelvic brim, the iliac crest and both
femoral heads and sits on them; the mirror map beside it does not. On F010 (PaxScan, half fan) at gantry 5° and
184° the contour lies on the femoral head, neck and greater trochanter, rings the obturator foramen and follows
both pubic rami, while the runner-up's contour runs through soft tissue. On F017 (RTI8643iL, the 86 × 43 cm
anisotropic imager) at gantry 145°, rescaled to isotropic millimetres, it follows the acetabular rim, the femoral
neck and the pubic ramus; the runner-up cuts across the pelvis. All three chains and the control agree with their
numbers. `varian/proof/<case>_<frame>_bone.png`.

### 2026-09-23 — the two abandoned assets: Dataset815 (the L6 set) and SpineSAC (six C-arms, no bone truth)

Jobs **40416580/40416581** (Dataset815 flags + resplit), **40416582/40416583/40416584** (SpineSAC SPINEPS →
body truth → project and score) and **40416585** (the last two Drive files), all resumable, all recorded in
`SchwingNet/results/JOBS_data_repairs.txt`. Box convention throughout is the **vertebral body** — the standing
decision of 2026-09-23 — which is why SpineSAC goes through SPINEPS and not TotalSegmentator.

**SpineSAC is now complete on disk: 5,492 of 5,492 (row: "2 of 5,492 Drive files never fetched").** Not a
network failure. Checked against `drive_tree.json` rather than assumed: `BMJH/SKANRAY/SCN 6/Output/AP/PD/AP_2D.txt`
and `.../AP/DD/AP_icp_2D.txt` are the **only two entries in the whole 5,492-file listing whose Drive-declared
size is 0**. The server answers HTTP 200 with an empty body — which is the file — and `os.path.getsize(tmp) > 0`
in `tools/spinesac_fetch.py` rejected the correct answer five times per attempt, twice (jobs 40411573, 40416361),
after which the job exited 2. An empty body is now accepted **only when the listing itself declares size 0**, so
a truncated transfer of a real file still fails loudly. Verified: both files on disk at 0 bytes, `file_list.txt`
= 5,492, `0 failed`.

**SpineSAC bone truth — the two prior attempts failed for reasons that are explicable and are not reasons to
stop (row: "no bone truth, nothing scored").** TotalSegmentator returned 0.0 ml for every vertebra on all three
sawbone CTs ("Crop is empty"), and the 150-HU fallback kept a single 8.4 ml component. Both are artefacts of the
phantom, not statements about it: a sawbone vertebra is a **hollow moulded shell**, so at any bone threshold it
fragments — 598 components at 150 HU on set1_A — and largest-connected-component is simply the wrong reduction.
The `ct_probe/` renders already on disk settle what is there: `A_thr150_sag.png` shows a complete,
ordinary-looking lumbar spine and sacrum, level by level. The bone is present, thresholdable and spine-shaped.
So SPINEPS is run over the three phantom CTs (40416582) and its output becomes per-level **vertebral body**
truth — instance id where the semantic mask is corpus 49/50, the definition `tools/body_masks.py` and
`tools/carmlspine_labels.py` already use — bound beside each CT on the CT's own grid, with a mid-sagittal and
mid-coronal overlay rendered per phantom (`or_ready/spinesac/truth/look/`) to be **looked at** before any box is
believed. `tools/spinesac_truth.py` gained a `spineps_body` branch to read it.

The geometry the projection needs was never the problem and is complete: all 59 scenarios carry a resolved
`APLP.txt` (two 3x4 matrices), a `gt_pose`, an `x0` and an ROI, with AP and LP processed frames at 1024x1024.
Only the segmentation was ever missing.

Two guards make the outcome legible instead of silent, and both are results rather than failures: the chain
stops at rc 4 if SPINEPS did not read the plastic phantom, and at rc 5 if no image validates — i.e. **if the
shipped poses do not put the boxes on the bones, that is the finding**, and the per-image overlays are the
evidence.

**SpineSAC device attribution is weaker than "six devices" suggests, and this is new.** Of the 59 scenarios only
**23 carry a device in a native DICOM header** (Philips AlluraXper 17, SIEMENS Healthineers 5, one blank). The
other **36 are attributed by folder name alone** (`2d_3dRL dataset/GE/...`, `.../SKANRAY/...`, `.../ZIEHM/...`,
plus `set1` and the two Thancopen sets), which the inventory records verbatim as "NO raw DICOM: folder name
only". Three of the six device identities therefore rest on a directory name — exactly the inference the
standing rule "key patients from metadata, not filenames" rejects. Any per-device card fitted from these frames
inherits that, and it must be said where the card is reported, not discovered later.

**Dataset815 — the phenotype flags were partly fiction, and the folds were built on them (rows: "1,176 cases
never consumed" and "phenotype flags from 327 of 374 reports").** `tools/merge_versefusion_corpus.py` reads
`has_L6`/`has_T13` for the CTSpinoPelvic1K half from the label volume, but for the VerSeFusion half from
`report_shard*.json`, which cover 327 of 374 converted cases; `bool(f.get("has_L6"))` then turns "no report"
into "no L6" without a word. Nothing raised, because every count printed was finite and looked reasonable — the
project's recurring failure exactly. `tools/d815_flags_and_integrity.py` now reads both flags off all 1,176
**label volumes** and rewrites `case_manifest.json` and `splits_final.json` through the **same**
`stratum()`/`make_folds()` the dataset was built with (imported, not reimplemented, so the folds differ only
because the flags do). Originals kept as `*.BEFORE.json`.

**Result, landed 03:17.** All 1,176 scanned. **L6 = 68 (18 ctsp1k + 50 versefusion), T13 = 6 (all
versefusion)** — against the 62 and 4 the dataset was built on. Exactly the eight cases the audit predicted
were misflagged, and no others: verse522, verse532, verse551, verse571, verse612, verse620 flipped to L6,
verse582 and verse601 to T13. The corrected VerSeFusion side, 50 and 6, **matches the VerSe published table** —
the check from outside the computation. The merge tool's own docstring ("L6 15 → 65") is wrong too. Memory
`l6-sample-count-and-counting-decoder` ("L6 62 (18 ctsp + 44 vf), T13 4") must be corrected to 68 and 6.

**Dataset815 — a link that resolves is not a file that reads, and CT 0533 is worse than a bad download.**
`prep815.sh`'s own guard is `find -xtype l`, which CT 0533 passes: it is present and its link resolves, but it
does not decompress (`EOFError: Compressed file ended before the end-of-stream marker was reached`), and it is
linked into the dataset as `COLONOG_378_0000.nii.gz`. `--verify_dataset_integrity` would have found it hours
into a twelve-hour job; the preflight found it in minutes and the resplit **refused** (rc 4) rather than write
a split over a corpus that cannot be read.

Reading the CT-0533 audit (job 40416363) afterwards changes what this gap *is*, and the change matters beyond
Dataset815. **It is not a broken download.** The grid file's sha256 is `20e673dc…` at 17,301,504 bytes, which
is exactly the size and LFS oid the HuggingFace v11 release declares for `ct/0533_ct.nii.gz`, and
`sha256sum -c SHA256SUMS.txt` passes 810/810 over the tree: **the published release object is itself
truncated**, so re-fetching can only reproduce it. And the older HF repo's copy (276,534,068 bytes, gzip-clean)
is **a different volume, not the same one intact** — CT shape (512, 593, 512) against the v11 label's
(512, 577, 512), affines not close — so dropping it in would put a CT and its label on different grids, the
exact class of error this project keeps paying for. It is a repair candidate, not a repair. Memory
`grid-ct-0533-truncated` calls it "a truncated download"; that diagnosis is wrong and should be corrected.

**Dataset815 — why the prep died twice, and what it needs today.** Jobs 40205238 and 40206171 both ended at
`fork/exec /usr/bin/singularity: no such file or directory` and nobody resubmitted. There is no
`/usr/bin/singularity` on these nodes; the `singularity` first on PATH is the user-local `~/.local/bin`
singularity-ce 4.0.2, whose unprivileged starter looks for the system binary — the identical failure the
one-shot run already hit and recorded. `slurm/prep815.sh` is now pinned to
`~/mambaforge/envs/nextflow/bin/singularity` (3.8.6), given the preflight above and a resume that exits 0 on an
already-preprocessed dataset. It is **not launched**, because it will refuse until CT 0533 is
resolved, and per the paragraph above that is an unresolved defect in the release rather than a re-fetch. Once
the CT-0533 repair concludes — a re-export of 0533 from source, or its exclusion with a written reason and the
release restated as 801 — the remaining two commands are, in order:
`sbatch -D ~/spinesurg-ct-nnunet slurm/d815_resplit.sh` (rewrites the folds from the already-cached flags;
nothing is rescanned) then `sbatch -D ~/spinesurg-ct-nnunet slurm/prep815.sh`.

**Dataset815 — what it is for, and whether the final corpus needs it.** It is the **L6 sample-count
experiment**, and only that: L6 pseudo-dice sat at exactly 0.000 for 27 epochs on Dataset813 with twelvefold
duplication of the 18 cases that carry an L6, which says the class is starved of *distinct* examples rather than
of gradient; Dataset815 joins VerSeFusion to raise the count and test that. It is a 3-D nnU-Net **CT
segmentation** dataset. Two facts bear on whether it is needed:

- **It adds no imaging.** Its 1,176 cases are symlinks into `~/data/CTSpinoPelvic1K/ct` (802) and `~/vf_813`
  (374). The render path reads **both of those trees directly** — `tools/make_drr_dataset.py --src ~/vf_813`
  and `tools/make_oblique_drr.py --src ~/vf_813 --ct-root ~/data/CTSpinoPelvic1K/ct` — and never reads
  Dataset815. So "1,176 cases never consumed" is a statement about a derived nnU-Net packaging, **not** about
  unused imaging: the underlying data is in the render banks and the 802 are in the SPINEPS pass (40416509).
- **No manuscript claim rests on it.** The L6 claims in `SchwingNet/CLAIMS_LEDGER.md` are about the 2-D
  morphology classifier (44.9 % L6) and the transitional inventory; the flagship ships a 2-D detector and a
  counting mechanism, not a 3-D CT segmenter. Nothing in the ledger cites the sample-count experiment.

The honest position is therefore: **the dataset is not needed for the paper as framed, and the reason is
scientific, not budgetary** — its imaging is fully used elsewhere and no claim depends on its result. It is not
declared superseded here, because that is a call for Greg and because the experiment is now cheap to run (the
flags and folds are repaired and the container is fixed); one CT repair stands between it and a launch. What
must not stand is the old description: with the flags read from the volumes the dataset holds **68 L6 and 6
T13**, not the 62 and 4 recorded in `merge_versefusion_corpus.py`'s output and in memory.

**Status of this repair.** Closed and verified: the SpineSAC fetch (5,492/5,492), the Dataset815 flag census
(1,176/1,176, eight flags corrected, one unreadable image found), the `prep815.sh` container fix, and a latent
3.04 TiB allocation in `tools/spinesac_ct.py` (`np.linalg.svd` on an N×3 with `full_matrices=True`, N =
646,657 — the crash that killed the fallback segmentation on 2026-09-22; now `full_matrices=False`, same
`vt[0]`). Queued and unattended: the SpineSAC SPINEPS → body → project → score chain, behind the 802-case
CTSpinoPelvic1K SPINEPS array on the GPUs. Blocked on another repair: the Dataset815 resplit and prep, on
CT 0533.

#### Addendum, 03:27 — two defects the SpineSAC bind tool would have shipped, found before the GPU queue cleared

The SPINEPS→body→project chain sits behind a three-hour GPU queue, so `tools/test_spinesac_bind_smoke.py`
(job 40416666/69/73, `slurm/spinesac_smoke.sh`) was written to exercise the mask construction and the overlay
renderer on synthetic volumes with known answers and on a real SPINEPS pair, rather than discover a bug on the
far side of a dependency chain. It found two, both of which would have produced finite, plausible numbers.

**1. SimpleITK cannot read SPINEPS output.** `sitk.ReadImage` on a SPINEPS mask raises *"ITK only supports
orthonormal direction cosines. No orthonormal definition found!"* — 1 of 25 masks in `~/bench_ts/spineps/native`
refused; nibabel opened 25 of 25. The bind tool was written on SimpleITK (the rest of the SpineSAC chain uses
it) and now reads with nibabel. It also **writes the body mask on the CT's own affine and header**, not on the
SPINEPS header, because a mask written on the SPINEPS header is likewise unreadable by the SimpleITK call in
`spinesac_truth.load_seg` — confirmed in the same test. Nothing is reoriented anywhere.

**2. "Instance × corpus 49/50" deletes the sacrum, and keeps the discs.** On a real pair (TS_s0001) every true
vertebra came back at a body fraction of **0.486–0.534** — about half the whole vertebra, which is what a
vertebral body should be, and an encouraging check on the definition — while **id 26, the sacrum, came back at
0.000**: SPINEPS assigns the sacrum no corpus sub-region, so the rule erases it. That matters here specifically,
because `spinesac_truth.py` uses the sacrum as its class 1 *and* as the structure that must land on the sacrum
for a projection convention to be accepted, and `spinesac_score.py` reports a sacrum-found rate. Had this
shipped, the convention search would have validated on vertebrae alone and the score would have read "sacrum
never found" — a wrong answer with no error. Separately, instance ids **≥ 100 are intervertebral discs**
(118–126 alongside 18–26) and also read 0.000. The tool now drops the discs, takes the corpus for the
vertebrae, and keeps the **sacrum whole**, recording which convention each level carries. The smoke test
asserts all three and passes (`SMOKE_OK failures=0`).

This is worth generalising beyond SpineSAC: the same 49/50 rule is what `tools/body_masks.py` applies to every
CT for the body-box convention (job 40416510). If the sacrum is a class in any render bank built that way, it
is being deleted there too, and any bank whose instance masks carry ids ≥ 100 is carrying discs as though they
were levels. Neither is checked anywhere at present.

#### The control that was run before the rescued masks were believed (job 40416665)

The masks the ladder rescued on a resampled grid came back carrying ids in the 100s (102–128) that **no mask
in the benchmark tree carries**: `tools/spineps_label_census.py` over 40 bench masks found 0 with such ids,
over the 10 rescued ones 10. The obvious reading — the resample is producing a different object — would have
made every rescued mask unshippable. It is wrong, and the way to know was to run BOTH rungs on the SAME six
CTs that SPINEPS already handles natively (`slurm/iso_rung_control.sh`, same models, same flags, same harvest,
only the grid differs):

| | cases | carrying ids ≥ 100 |
|---|---|---|
| bench tree, produced 2026-09-19 | 40 | **0** |
| control, native rung, produced today | 5 | **5** |
| control, isotropic rung, produced today | 6 | **6** |

So the extra ids are not the resample. They are **intervertebral discs**: every high id is exactly a vertebra
id plus 100, one per interspace, carrying 4–11 % of the vertebral voxel count (CTRL_s0004: vertebrae
7–24 and 26, discs 107–124, 250,606 vertebra voxels against 27,745 disc voxels). SPINEPS emits them on every
run made today and on no run made at the bench, so **the SPINEPS/TPTBox environment changed between the two**
and the corpus now holds two mask conventions.

Two things follow, and the second is not mine to act on:
1. **The isotropic rescue is faithful.** On CTRL_s0002 the native and isotropic runs of the same CT agree to
   0.1 % (27,901 vs 27,867 vertebra voxels; 1,286 vs 1,281 disc voxels) with identical id sets. A mask the
   ladder rescued can stand beside a native one.
2. **Every SPINEPS mask made from now on carries disc ids, and the 1,484 bench masks do not** — including the
   802 CTSpinoPelvic1K masks of job 40416509 and the v3 masks of 40416540. `tools/body_masks.py` intersects
   the instance mask with the semantic corpus (49/50), and a disc is not corpus, so a disc contributes ~0 body
   voxels: the body MASKS and the body BOXES are unaffected (`body_boxes` bridges by name, and 1xx has no
   VerSe name). What *is* affected is any count taken off these masks — `n_levels` in `<case>_body.json` is
   inflated by roughly one per interspace, and a levels-per-case or L6/T13 census that counts unique ids will
   be wrong on the new masks and right on the old ones. **Not fixed here**: `tools/body_masks.py` is the tool
   job 40416510 is running, and this repair does not touch it. The one-line fix when that job is done is to
   drop ids ≥ 100 before counting levels, and to re-state whether the 1,484 bench masks should be regenerated
   so that one environment produced the whole corpus.

### 2026-09-23 — COBRA Elekta: the display window's field was air, and the eval charged off-panel truth (A31)

**Finding** (`SchwingNet/results/cobra_window/DIAGNOSIS.md`, computed over all 3,480 exported cobra_elekta
frames of 150 cases). The export passed `deepfluoro_anchor.window_or` a field of every pixel with signal; on
half-fan laterals that field is up to 57 % air (median air share 0.071 over 542 AP frames, 0.152 over 2,207
obliques, 0.258 over 731 laterals), so p1/p5 of the percentile ladder collapsed to air and the body was
squeezed into grey 126–239 (shipped lateral body p5/p50/p95 = 69/175/222) — the "looks like shit" Figure 4a
Elekta tile. Separately, 864 of 1,502 test truth boxes carry `visible_frac` < 1 because the offset half-fan
panel sees a 56.8 mm short-side band at the isocentre (median over 150 cases), and `tools/realxray_eval.py`
never read that field: every partly visible box stood in the recall denominator, biasing the quoted Elekta
recall DOWN. The card fit itself survives: recomputed on the same 335 calibration pairs, median R² 0.976
body-only vs 0.980 full-field (`r2_body_vs_field.json`) — the R² was never an air artifact.

**Repair** (commit 7631a1c + this commit; `SchwingNet/results/cobra_window/FIX.md` for the full table).
`realxray_cobra_export.py --window-region body` windows over attenuation >= 0.25 inside the field (guarded by
a max(2000 px, 2 %) floor, stated per frame in meta) and rebuilt the bank as `realxray/cobra_elekta_v3` —
same 150 cases, 24 frames each, same splits and truth; the v1 bank the arms trained on is untouched
(150/150 case JSONs on disk; finalize 40418922 pending). `realxray_eval.py --min-vis` moves truth below the
threshold to IGNORE with `pooled.ignored_truth` reported; the pre-existing corpus threshold is 0.5 (`MIN_VIS`
in all three exporters and the keep rule in `docs/REAL_XRAY_DATASETS_SPEC.md`). The same air-in-the-field
defect was fixed opt-in (defaults preserve every shipped bank) in the DeepFluoro, Geneva, C-ArmLSpine and
SpineSAC exporters, in `device_style.style` (so a styled set paired with v3 gets identical treatment) and in
`device_fit.fig_pairs` (`DF_WINDOW_REGION=body`, panels only — the card statistics stay fixed while the A32
refit runs). Gated: rescore 40418931 (3 detectors x {v1,v3} x min-vis {0.0, 0.75}) + 40419583 (min-vis 0.5)
must land before any Elekta recall, Figure 3d/4a rebuild, or the learning curve's Elekta arm is quoted.
